People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I came in 'cause my family and friends, they keep waving these bank papers in my face, saying it proves nothing was taken. But I ain't stupid. For over a year now I’ve known they’re hiding my money. They show me these papers, and the ink on 'em is always smudged. Reminds me of when I used to try and write with those cheap pens from the dollar store. The one over on 5th Street, you know? They closed it down a while back. It's a real shame 'cause they had good peppermint candy. 

But yeah, they're using all this stuff to cover it up—fake bills, these fundraisers they keep bringing up, even trip plans. My aunt keeps talking about planning a trip to Ohio. Who even goes to Ohio for fun? It's just corn and flat roads everywhere. I rode through there once and the radio only played country music for like four hours straight. 

But I know it's all just a trick to hide what they did with my money, so I just have to keep confronting them about it. I'm gonna keep at it until they finally tell the truth. Did you see the magazines out in the waiting room, by the way? They're all from like three years ago.

I just want them to stop lying to me about my money, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices when no one else was around?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear any voices. Just my own thoughts, you know? And the TV, of course. I leave the TV on a lot, mostly for background noise. I was watching this documentary the other day about deep-sea fishing. My uncle used to have a boat, a little aluminum thing he took out on the lake. We went out once and the motor died right in the middle. We had to paddle back with these tiny plastic oars. Took us like three hours, and I got the worst sunburn on my shoulders. \n\nBut anyway, no, no voices. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I rode through there once and the radio only played country music for like four hours straight. \n\nBut I know it's all just a trick to hide what they did with my money, so I just have to keep confronting them about it. I'm gonna keep at it until they finally tell the truth. Did you see the magazines out in the waiting room, by the way? They're all from like three years ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your mood was unusually high, energized, or irritable for several days?\n\nPatient: Unusually high? Like, energized? No, I don't... I don't get like that. I'm just me. I mean, sometimes I get a little jittery if I have too much caffeine, I guess. I was drinking those big energy drinks for a while, the ones with the green claws on the can? My neighbor used to leave the empty cans all over the front steps. He’s so messy, I swear. He leaves his trash everywhere and lets his dog dig up the yard. It's a little terrier mix, cute, but it barks at every single car that drives by. Drives me absolutely crazy, especially when the mailman comes. \n\nBut no, I don't have days where I'm all energized for no reason or anything like that. I just want them to stop lying to me about my money, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices when no one else was around?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear any voices. Just my own thoughts, you know? And the TV, of course. I leave the TV on a lot, mostly for background noise. I was watching this documentary the other day about deep-sea fishing. Did you know they can catch crabs that are like, three feet wide? It's terrifying. I could never go out on the ocean like that. The water is just so dark and you have no idea what's underneath you. My uncle used to have a boat, a little aluminum thing he took out on the lake. We went out once and the motor died right in the middle. We had to paddle back with these tiny plastic oars. Took us like three hours, and I got the worst sunburn on my shoulders. \n\nBut anyway, no, no voices. Just my family lying to my face about my bank accounts.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about your money ever led you to stop working or handling your day-to-day responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Stop working? Other than the arguments with them about the money, I still handle my own meals, get myself down to the county clinic, and make it to my assistance appointments. I keep my things in order the exact same way I always have. I'm very organized, actually. I keep all my important papers in this little blue folder. It's got little daisies printed on it. I found it at a yard sale a few years ago. The lady running it was selling all these old records, too. Tons of them, like, big stacks of vinyl. I don't even have a record player, but I almost bought one just because the covers looked so neat. They had that old, dusty smell, you know? Like an antique shop. They just want you to think I can't so they can keep stealing from me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever given you a convincing explanation that your money is actually where it should be?\n\nPatient: Convincing? You know, the ones they print on that really thin, cheap paper? It always gives me paper cuts. I got a terrible paper cut just last week opening a letter. It bled right onto the envelope, and I had to use one of those little round bandaids. The ones that never actually stick to your finger right? Messages? Like, through the TV or something? No, nothing like that. Nobody's trying to control my thoughts. Ignore it? No, I can't just ignore it. I mean, how could I? I came in here because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know better. Mistaken? Safe? No, no, they're not mistaken. They know exactly what they're doing. I told you, I came in here because they keep shoving those bank papers at me, acting like that proves my money is safe. But it doesn't prove anything! I've known for over a year now that they're hiding it. They're using all those things—the bills, the school fundraisers, the big trip plans—just to cover up that they're draining my accounts. 

Speaking of accounts, I tried to log into my email account the other day, and I couldn't remember my password. It's so annoying how every website wants a different password now. You need a capital letter, a number, a special symbol... I used to just use my dog's name, Buster. Buster was a golden retriever. He was the sweetest dog, but he chewed up everything. I got them for Christmas from my aunt, the one who's always talking about going on cruises. She went on a cruise to Alaska once and brought me back this little totem pole carved out of wood. Did you know they can catch crabs that are like, three feet wide? It's terrifying. I could never go out on the ocean like that. The water is just so dark and you have no idea what's underneath you. My uncle used to have a boat, a little aluminum thing he took out on the lake. We went out once and the motor died right in the middle. We had to paddle back with these tiny plastic oars. Took us like three hours, and I got the worst sunburn on my shoulders. \n\nBut anyway, no, no voices. Just my family lying to my face about my bank accounts.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about your money ever led you to stop working or handling your day-to-day responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Stop working? Well, I don't really have a job right now anyway, but handling my stuff? No, no, I still do everything I need to do. Other than the arguments with them about the money, I still handle my own meals, get myself down to the county clinic, and make it to my assistance appointments. I keep my things in order the exact same way I always have. I'm very organized, actually. I keep all my important papers in this little blue folder. It's got little daisies printed on it. I found it at a yard sale a few years ago. The lady running it was selling all these old records, too. Tons of them, like, big stacks of vinyl. I don't even have a record player, but I almost bought one just because the covers looked so neat. They had that old, dusty smell, you know? Like an antique shop. I love antique shops, but they always charge too much for old glass bottles. I don't know why people collect those anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I still take care of my day-to-day stuff just fine. They just want you to think I can't so they can keep stealing from me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever given you a convincing explanation that your money is actually where it should be?\n\nPatient: Convincing? No, never. I mean, my family and friends, they keep waving these bank papers in my face, saying it proves nothing was taken. Bank papers. You know, the ones they print on that really thin, cheap paper? It always gives me paper cuts. I got a terrible paper cut just last week opening a letter. It bled right onto the envelope, and I had to use one of those little round bandaids. The ones that never actually stick to your finger right? My cousin bought a whole box of them for her kid's school fundraiser. \n\nWhich, by the way, speaking of fundraisers, that's exactly what they do! They use these fake fundraisers, and bills, and all these big plans for trips to cover up where my money is actually going. For over a year now, I’ve known they’re hiding it. Like my cousin talking about a trip to Florida. Florida! It's so humid there. I went to Florida when I was little and my hair just frizzed up like a poodle. I don't even like the beach anyway, the sand gets everywhere. You find it in your shoes for months, it's disgusting. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't believe their little papers. I just have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that anyone is trying to send you special messages or control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Messages? Like, through the TV or something? No, nothing like that. Nobody's trying to control my thoughts. My thoughts are my own, just... just trying to figure out how they're moving my money around. You know, speaking of moving things around, I was trying to reorganize my kitchen cabinets yesterday. I have all these mismatched Tupperware containers, and I can never find the right lids. It's so frustrating. I spent like an hour just matching lids to bowls. I found this one bowl that I swear I haven't seen since I was a teenager. It's got these little strawberries painted on the side. My grandma used to have a whole set just like it. She used to make the best strawberry jam. We'd go picking in the summer, and my hands would be stained red for days. It was so hot out there in the fields, but she always wore this big straw hat with a green ribbon. \n\nBut no, nobody is controlling my thoughts or sending me messages. They're just lying to me about my money.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been able to completely ignore or dismiss the belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: Ignore it? No, I can't just ignore it. I mean, how could I? I came in here because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know better. For over a year, I've known they're hiding my money. They use bills, and those fundraisers I was telling you about, and all these trip plans to cover it up. Like, they're always planning trips. My uncle was talking about going to the Grand Canyon next summer. The Grand Canyon! I saw a documentary about it once. It's just a big hole in the ground, really. Why would anyone want to go stare at a hole? And the mules they ride down there, I feel so bad for them. Carrying all those people in the heat. It gets so hot in the desert. I bought a cactus once for my windowsill, thinking it would be easy to take care of, but I actually watered it too much and it turned to mush. It smelled awful. I had to throw the whole pot away, which was a shame because it was a really pretty terracotta pot with little painted suns on it. \n\nBut anyway, no, I can't dismiss it. I have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth about what they're doing with my money.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any chance at all that your family might be mistaken and your money is actually safe?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? Safe? No, no, they're not mistaken. They know exactly what they're doing. I told you, I came in here because they keep shoving those bank papers at me, acting like that proves my money is safe. But it doesn't prove anything! I've known for over a year now that they're hiding it. They're using all those things—the bills, the school fundraisers, the big trip plans—just to cover up that they're draining my accounts. \n\nSpeaking of accounts, I tried to log into my email account the other day, and I couldn't remember my password. It's so annoying how every website wants a different password now. You need a capital letter, a number, a special symbol... I used to just use my dog's name, Buster. Buster was a golden retriever. I got them for Christmas from my aunt, the one who's always talking about going on cruises. She went on a cruise to Alaska once and brought me back this little totem pole carved out of wood. It sits on my dresser, right next to my jewelry box. \n\nBut anyway, no, they aren't mistaken. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, it's been going on since my income dropped, which was about a year and two months ago. I know exactly when it was because I had just gotten my big winter coat out of the closet. It's this heavy navy blue puffy coat I found at a thrift store. Thrift stores are getting so expensive lately, though. You go in there and they want fifteen dollars for a used shirt! I saw this one sweater there that had a big cat knitted on the front, but the cat's eyes were these shiny green buttons. It looked so creepy, like it was staring right through you. I hate it when things stare. My neighbor has this orange cat that just sits on the fence and watches my house all day. I think it's waiting for the birds at my birdfeeder. I try to put out those black sunflower seeds for the blue jays, but the squirrels always get them first. They just sit there stuffing their cheeks until their faces look like little furry balloons. 

But yeah, about a year and two months ago, right when my money went down, that's when they started taking it and hiding it from me.

It sits on my dresser, right next to my jewelry box. \n\nBut anyway, no, they aren't mistaken. They're lying, and I'm just going to keep confronting them, every single day, until they finally admit the truth and give it back.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been convinced that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on since my income dropped, which was about a year and two months ago. I know exactly when it was because I had just gotten my big winter coat out of the closet. It's this heavy navy blue puffy coat I found at a thrift store. Thrift stores are getting so expensive lately, though. You go in there and they want fifteen dollars for a used shirt! I saw this one sweater there that had a big cat knitted on the front, but the cat's eyes were these shiny green buttons. It looked so creepy, like it was staring right through you. I try to put out those black sunflower seeds for the blue jays, but the squirrels always get them first. They just sit there stuffing their cheeks until their faces look like little furry balloons. \n\nBut yeah, about a year and two months ago, right when my money went down, that's when they started taking it and hiding it from me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period of at least one week when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt fine?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Sleep? No, not really. I sleep about the same as I always do. Maybe a little less lately because I'm so worried about what they're doing with my money, but not like... not needing sleep and feeling fine. I always feel tired if I don't get my eight hours. Although, my mattress is getting really old. It's one of those spring ones, and there's this one spring right in the middle that pokes me in the back. I tried flipping it over, but it's just as bad on the other side. I should probably get a memory foam one. I saw a commercial for them the other night while I was watching TV. I like watching those late-night infomercials, the ones where they try to sell you the weird kitchen gadgets. Like that thing that chops an onion in one second? It looks so easy on TV, but I bet it's a nightmare to clean. All those little blades. I cut my finger on a cheese grater once, right on the knuckle. It bled for an hour. I had to wrap it in a paper towel and hold it above my head. Blood is so hard to get out of clothes, too. I spilled some on my favorite white t-shirt and had to soak it in club soda. 

But no, I haven't had anything like that with my sleep. Just normal, I guess. Well, as normal as it can be right now.

No, I can't just ignore it. I mean, how could I? I came in here because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know better. For over a year, I've known they're hiding my money. They use bills, and those fundraisers I was telling you about, and all these trip plans to cover it up. Like, they're always planning trips. My uncle was talking about going to the Grand Canyon next summer. The Grand Canyon! I saw a documentary about it once. It's just a big hole in the ground, really. Why would anyone want to go stare at a hole? And the mules they ride down there, I feel so bad for them. Carrying all those people in the heat. It gets so hot in the desert. I bought a cactus once for my windowsill, thinking it would be easy to take care of, but I actually watered it too much and it turned to mush. It smelled awful. I had to throw the whole pot away, which was a shame because it was a really pretty terracotta pot with little painted suns on it. \n\nBut anyway, no, I can't dismiss it. I have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth about what they're doing with my money.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any chance at all that your family might be mistaken and your money is actually safe?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? They're using all those things—the bills, the school fundraisers, the big trip plans—just to cover up that they're draining my accounts. \n\nSpeaking of accounts, I tried to log into my email account the other day, and I couldn't remember my password. It's so annoying how every website wants a different password now. You need a capital letter, a number, a special symbol... I used to just use my dog's name, Buster. Buster was a golden retriever. He was the sweetest dog, but he chewed up everything. He chewed up my favorite pair of slippers once. They were those fuzzy pink ones with the little bunny ears on them. I got them for Christmas from my aunt, the one who's always talking about going on cruises. She went on a cruise to Alaska once and brought me back this little totem pole carved out of wood. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, people don't usually say that to me. I mean, I think I make perfect sense. Sometimes people don't listen, though. Like the lady at the grocery store checkout yesterday. I was trying to tell her that the price on the apples was wrong, because the sign back in the produce section said they were on sale for ninety-nine cents a pound, but they were ringing up as a dollar forty-nine. She just looked at me like I was speaking a different language. I hate those self-checkout machines, too. They always yell at you about an unexpected item in the bagging area. It's like, I just put my bread down, calm down! Bread is so expensive now, too. I used to get that nice sourdough from the bakery down the street, but they closed last month. The owner retired and moved to Florida. Florida seems too hot to me. All that humidity, it makes your hair frizz up. My sister went to Florida once for a vacation and she said she got sunburned on the very first day. She had to sit in the hotel room with aloe vera all over her back. Aloe vera is a funny plant, isn't it? It looks like a cactus but it's squishy inside. I tried to grow one on my windowsill, but I think I watered it too much and it turned brown and died. I'm not very good with plants. 

But no, nobody tells me I'm hard to follow. I think I explain things pretty clearly.

I don't even have a record player, but I almost bought one just because the covers looked so neat. They had that old, dusty smell, you know? Like an antique shop. I love antique shops, but they always charge too much for old glass bottles. I don't know why people collect those anyway. \n\nBut yeah, I still take care of my day-to-day stuff just fine. They just want you to think I can't so they can keep stealing from me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever given you a convincing explanation that your money is actually where it should be?\n\nPatient: Convincing? No, never. I mean, my family and friends, they keep waving these bank papers in my face, saying it proves nothing was taken. Bank papers. You know, the ones they print on that really thin, cheap paper? It always gives me paper cuts. I got a terrible paper cut just last week opening a letter. It bled right onto the envelope, and I had to use one of those little round bandaids. The ones that never actually stick to your finger right? My cousin bought a whole box of them for her kid's school fundraiser. \n\nWhich, by the way, speaking of fundraisers, that's exactly what they do! Like my cousin talking about a trip to Florida. Florida! It's so humid there. I went to Florida when I was little and my hair just frizzed up like a poodle. I don't even like the beach anyway, the sand gets everywhere. You find it in your shoes for months, it's disgusting. \n\nBut anyway, no, I don't believe their little papers. I just have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that anyone is trying to send you special messages or control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Messages? Like, through the TV or something? No, nothing like that. Nobody's trying to control my thoughts. My thoughts are my own, just... just trying to figure out how they're moving my money around. You know, speaking of moving things around, I was trying to reorganize my kitchen cabinets yesterday. I have all these mismatched Tupperware containers, and I can never find the right lids. It's so frustrating. I spent like an hour just matching lids to bowls. I found this one bowl that I swear I haven't seen since I was a teenager. It's got these little strawberries painted on the side. My grandma used to have a whole set just like it. She used to make the best strawberry jam. We'd go picking in the summer, and my hands would be stained red for days. It was so hot out there in the fields, but she always wore this big straw hat with a green ribbon. \n\nBut no, nobody is controlling my thoughts or sending me messages. They're just lying to me about my money.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been able to completely ignore or dismiss the belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: Ignore it? No, I can't just ignore it. I mean, how could I? I came in here because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I know better. For over a year, I've known they're hiding my money. They use bills, and those fundraisers I was telling you about, and all these trip plans to cover it up. Like, they're always planning trips. My uncle was talking about going to the Grand Canyon next summer. The Grand Canyon! I saw a documentary about it once. It's just a big hole in the ground, really. Why would anyone want to go stare at a hole? And the mules they ride down there, I feel so bad for them. Carrying all those people in the heat. It gets so hot in the desert. I bought a cactus once for my windowsill, thinking it would be easy to take care of, but I actually watered it too much and it turned to mush. It smelled awful. I had to throw the whole pot away, which was a shame because it was a really pretty terracotta pot with little painted suns on it. \n\nBut anyway, no, I can't dismiss it. I have to keep confronting them until they finally tell the truth about what they're doing with my money.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any chance at all that your family might be mistaken and your money is actually safe?\n\nPatient: Mistaken? Safe? No, no, they're not mistaken. They know exactly what they're doing. I told you, I came in here because they keep shoving those bank papers at me, acting like that proves my money is safe. But it doesn't prove anything! I've known for over a year now that they're hiding it. They're using all those things—the bills, the school fundraisers, the big trip plans—just to cover up that they're draining my accounts. \n\nSpeaking of accounts, I tried to log into my email account the other day, and I couldn't remember my password. It's so annoying how every website wants a different password now. You need a capital letter, a number, a special symbol... I used to just use my dog's name, Buster. Buster was a golden retriever. He was the sweetest dog, but he chewed up everything. He chewed up my favorite pair of slippers once. They were those fuzzy pink ones with the little bunny ears on them. I got them for Christmas from my aunt, the one who's always talking about going on cruises. She went on a cruise to Alaska once and brought me back this little totem pole carved out of wood. It sits on my dresser, right next to my jewelry box. \n\nBut anyway, no, they aren't mistaken. They're lying, and I'm just going to keep confronting them, every single day, until they finally admit the truth and give it back.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been convinced that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on since my income dropped, which was about a year and two months ago. I know exactly when it was because I had just gotten my big winter coat out of the closet. It's this heavy navy blue puffy coat I found at a thrift store. Thrift stores are getting so expensive lately, though. You go in there and they want fifteen dollars for a used shirt! I saw this one sweater there that had a big cat knitted on the front, but the cat's eyes were these shiny green buttons. It looked so creepy, like it was staring right through you. I hate it when things stare. My neighbor has this orange cat that just sits on the fence and watches my house all day. I think it's waiting for the birds at my birdfeeder. I try to put out those black sunflower seeds for the blue jays, but the squirrels always get them first. They just sit there stuffing their cheeks until their faces look like little furry balloons. \n\nBut yeah, about a year and two months ago, right when my money went down, that's when they started taking it and hiding it from me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt fine?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, not really. I sleep about the same as I always do. Maybe a little less lately because I'm so worried about what they're doing with my money, but not like... not needing sleep and feeling fine. I always feel tired if I don't get my eight hours. Although, my mattress is getting really old. It's one of those spring ones, and there's this one spring right in the middle that pokes me in the back. I tried flipping it over, but it's just as bad on the other side. I should probably get a memory foam one. I saw a commercial for them the other night while I was watching TV. I like watching those late-night infomercials, the ones where they try to sell you the weird kitchen gadgets. Like that thing that chops an onion in one second? It looks so easy on TV, but I bet it's a nightmare to clean. All those little blades. I cut my finger on a cheese grater once, right on the knuckle. It bled for an hour. I had to wrap it in a paper towel and hold it above my head. Blood is so hard to get out of clothes, too. I spilled some on my favorite white t-shirt and had to soak it in club soda. \n\nBut no, I haven't had anything like that with my sleep. Just normal, I guess. Well, as normal as it can be right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced smells, tastes, or physical sensations that other people couldn’t detect?\n\nPatient: Smells or tastes? No, I don't think so. I mean, my sense of smell is pretty normal. Although, I did smell something burning yesterday, but it turned out my neighbor was just having a barbecue. They use that cheap charcoal, the kind that comes in the big red bags? It always smells like gasoline when they first light it up. I don't understand why people don't just use a gas grill. It's so much cleaner. My dad used to have a gas grill, this huge silver one with three different burners. He would make hot dogs on it every Fourth of July. I love hot dogs, but only if they have that really yellow mustard on them. Not the spicy brown kind, that stuff just tastes like dirt to me. I accidentally put it on a sandwich once when I was at a deli. The deli was right next to this old shoe repair shop, and the whole street smelled like leather and polish. It's a nice smell, leather. Reminds me of this jacket I used to have... but anyway, no, I haven't felt or smelled anything weird that wasn't actually there.\n\nDoctor: When you’re talking with other people, do they ever tell you that your speech is hard to follow or doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: No, people don't usually say that to me. I mean, I think I make perfect sense. Sometimes people don't listen, though. Like the lady at the grocery store checkout yesterday. It’s not impossible, it’s just... it’s them. My family, my friends, they’re the ones doing it. I came in here because they keep shoving these bank papers in my face, telling me it proves nothing is missing, but I know. For over a year, I’ve known they’re hiding my money. The airlines are always losing people's luggage. My cousin flew to Chicago once and her suitcase ended up in Dallas. She had to wear the same clothes for three days. She bought this really ugly tourist t-shirt from the hotel gift shop just to have something clean. Gift shops are such a rip-off, anyway. They charge you five dollars for a tiny bottle of water. I started carrying my own water bottle everywhere, one of those metal ones that keeps the ice cold all day. But you have to wash the straw part really well or it gets moldy. I use a little tiny brush for it, the kind they make for baby bottles. Babies are so much work. I don't know how people do it, waking up every two hours. 

But yeah, they use all these fake expenses to hide what they’re taking, and I just have to keep confronting them until they finally admit the truth. They think I don't see it, but I do.

It's so frustrating. I spent like an hour just matching lids to bowls. I found this one bowl that I swear I haven't seen since I was a teenager. It's got these little strawberries painted on the side. My grandma used to have a whole set just like it. She used to make the best strawberry jam. He was the sweetest dog, but he chewed up everything. He chewed up my favorite pair of slippers once. They were those fuzzy pink ones with the little bunny ears on them. I got them for Christmas from my aunt, the one who's always talking about going on cruises. She went on a cruise to Alaska once and brought me back this little totem pole carved out of wood. They're lying, and I'm just going to keep confronting them, every single day, until they finally admit the truth and give it back.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been convinced that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on since my income dropped, which was about a year and two months ago. Thrift stores are getting so expensive lately, though. You go in there and they want fifteen dollars for a used shirt! I saw this one sweater there that had a big cat knitted on the front, but the cat's eyes were these shiny green buttons. It looked so creepy, like it was staring right through you. I hate it when things stare. My neighbor has this orange cat that just sits on the fence and watches my house all day. I think it's waiting for the birds at my birdfeeder. I try to put out those black sunflower seeds for the blue jays, but the squirrels always get them first. They just sit there stuffing their cheeks until their faces look like little furry balloons. \n\nBut yeah, about a year and two months ago, right when my money went down, that's when they started taking it and hiding it from me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least one week when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt fine?\n\nPatient: Sleep? I always feel tired if I don't get my eight hours. Although, my mattress is getting really old. It's one of those spring ones, and there's this one spring right in the middle that pokes me in the back. I tried flipping it over, but it's just as bad on the other side. I should probably get a memory foam one. I saw a commercial for them the other night while I was watching TV. I like watching those late-night infomercials, the ones where they try to sell you the weird kitchen gadgets. Like that thing that chops an onion in one second? It looks so easy on TV, but I bet it's a nightmare to clean. All those little blades. I cut my finger on a cheese grater once, right on the knuckle. It bled for an hour. I had to wrap it in a paper towel and hold it above my head. Blood is so hard to get out of clothes, too. I spilled some on my favorite white t-shirt and had to soak it in club soda. \n\nBut no, I haven't had anything like that with my sleep. Just normal, I guess. Well, as normal as it can be right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced smells, tastes, or physical sensations that other people couldn’t detect?\n\nPatient: Smells or tastes? No, I don't think so. I mean, my sense of smell is pretty normal. Although, I did smell something burning yesterday, but it turned out my neighbor was just having a barbecue. They use that cheap charcoal, the kind that comes in the big red bags? It always smells like gasoline when they first light it up. I don't understand why people don't just use a gas grill. It's so much cleaner. My dad used to have a gas grill, this huge silver one with three different burners. He would make hot dogs on it every Fourth of July. I love hot dogs, but only if they have that really yellow mustard on them. Not the spicy brown kind, that stuff just tastes like dirt to me. I accidentally put it on a sandwich once when I was at a deli. The deli was right next to this old shoe repair shop, and the whole street smelled like leather and polish. It's a nice smell, leather. Reminds me of this jacket I used to have... but anyway, no, I haven't felt or smelled anything weird that wasn't actually there.\n\nDoctor: When you’re talking with other people, do they ever tell you that your speech is hard to follow or doesn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: No, people don't usually say that to me. I mean, I think I make perfect sense. Sometimes people don't listen, though. Like the lady at the grocery store checkout yesterday. I was trying to tell her that the price on the apples was wrong, because the sign back in the produce section said they were on sale for ninety-nine cents a pound, but they were ringing up as a dollar forty-nine. She just looked at me like I was speaking a different language. I hate those self-checkout machines, too. They always yell at you about an unexpected item in the bagging area. It's like, I just put my bread down, calm down! Bread is so expensive now, too. I used to get that nice sourdough from the bakery down the street, but they closed last month. The owner retired and moved to Florida. Florida seems too hot to me. All that humidity, it makes your hair frizz up. My sister went to Florida once for a vacation and she said she got sunburned on the very first day. She had to sit in the hotel room with aloe vera all over her back. Aloe vera is a funny plant, isn't it? It looks like a cactus but it's squishy inside. I tried to grow one on my windowsill, but I think I watered it too much and it turned brown and died. I'm not very good with plants. \n\nBut no, nobody tells me I'm hard to follow. I think I explain things pretty clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything unusual or impossible is happening to your money, or is it more that someone you know is secretly taking it?\n\nPatient: It’s not impossible, it’s just... it’s them. My family, my friends, they’re the ones doing it. I came in here because they keep shoving these bank papers in my face, telling me it proves nothing is missing, but I know. For over a year, I’ve known they’re hiding my money. They try to cover it up with all these excuses—like, oh, this bill had to be paid, or we need to donate to this fundraiser, or we’re planning a trip. A trip! Who plans a trip right now? The airlines are always losing people's luggage. My cousin flew to Chicago once and her suitcase ended up in Dallas. She had to wear the same clothes for three days. She bought this really ugly tourist t-shirt from the hotel gift shop just to have something clean. Gift shops are such a rip-off, anyway. They charge you five dollars for a tiny bottle of water. I started carrying my own water bottle everywhere, one of those metal ones that keeps the ice cold all day. But you have to wash the straw part really well or it gets moldy. I use a little tiny brush for it, the kind they make for baby bottles. Babies are so much work. I don't know how people do it, waking up every two hours. \n\nBut yeah, they use all these fake expenses to hide what they’re taking, and I just have to keep confronting them until they finally admit the truth. They think I don't see it, but I do.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
